In May 2022, Allscripts sold its hospital and large physician practice business (Sunrise, Paragon, TouchWorks, dbMotion) to Harris Computer / Constellation Software for ~$700M; that segment became **Altera Digital Health**. The remaining ambulatory EHR, practice management, patient engagement, and data analytics businesses were formally rebranded as **Veradigm** in January 2023.\n     9→\n    10→Veradigm has ~2,450 employees and generates approximately $585M in annual revenue across two segments: Provider (~77% of revenue, covering EHR, practice management, patient engagement, and revenue cycle management) and Payer & Life Sciences (~23%, covering data analytics and real-world evidence). The company claims over 180,000 physician users. It holds approximately 3.6% of the U.S. ambulatory EHR market — significant in the small-to-mid-size independent practice segment, though small relative to Epic (~44%).\n    11→\n    12→Veradigm has been in financial turmoil since March 2023 when it disclosed a ~$20M revenue overstatement (2021-2022), triggering an SEC investigation, executive departures, and Nasdaq delisting in February 2024. Shares now trade OTC under ticker MDRX. The company continues operating and plans to relist after completing financial restatements, targeted for 2026. Despite the turmoil, Veradigm acquired Koha Health (RCM, orthopedic focus) in early 2024 and ScienceIO (AI data platform) for $140M.\n    13→\n    14→Notably, Veradigm also owns **Practice Fusion** (a separate, simpler cloud-native EHR for small practices) and **FollowMyHealth** (a patient engagement platform). These are distinct ONC-certified products. This report focuses on **Veradigm EHR**, the flagship ambulatory EHR product.\n    15→\n    16→## Product: Veradigm EHR\n    17→\n    18→CHPL ID: 11763\n    19→\n    20→### What It Is\n    21→\n    22→Veradigm EHR is the direct successor to **Allscripts Professional EHR** (also known as \"Allscripts Pro\"), which itself descended from the \"HealthMatics\" EHR. It is a full-featured ambulatory EHR targeting small-to-mid-size independent physician practices and multi-location, multi-specialty organizations. It is **not** the same product as Allscripts TouchWorks (which went to Altera Digital Health and serves larger enterprises).\n    23→\n    24→The certified product (Veradigm EHR version 26) has an extensive ONC certification profile with 40+ criteria spanning clinical data management (a)(1)-(a)(15), care coordination (b)(1)-(b)(11), clinical quality measures (c)(1)-(c)(3), patient access (e)(1), public health reporting (f)(1)-(f)(7), FHIR APIs (g)(7)-(g)(10), and Direct messaging (h)(1). This breadth indicates a comprehensive clinical system.\n    25→\n    26→Veradigm EHR is the clinical core, but the full product ecosystem includes **Veradigm Practice Management** (billing/scheduling — separate but integrated product), **FollowMyHealth** (patient portal — EHR-agnostic, separately certified), **Veradigm ePrescribe** (also available standalone), and the **DORN** diagnostic ordering/results network. The certified EHR module is a component of this broader platform.\n    27→\n    28→### Users & Market\n    29→\n    30→**Target users:** Physicians, clinical staff, nurses, and practice administrators in ambulatory settings. The product supports solo practices through multi-site groups with 50+ providers.\n    31→\n    32→**Specialties:** Veradigm claims support for \"nearly every medical specialty\" with preloaded templates and protocols. Explicitly listed specialties include: cardiology, orthopedics, neurology, dermatology, gastroenterology, endocrinology, ENT, urology, family practice, internal medicine, pediatrics, OB/GYN, psychiatry, urgent care, oncology, pulmonology, rheumatology, allergy/immunology, occupational medicine, podiatry, physical therapy, and more.\n    33→\n    34→**Market position:** ~3.6% ambulatory market share. Ranked #1 by Black Book Research (2025) for ambulatory EHR in family practice and primary care. Over 180,000 physician users across the full Veradigm platform (not all on Veradigm EHR specifically — some use Practice Fusion).\n    35→\n    36→**Deployment:** Cloud-based (primary), on-premise, or hybrid. Available via web browser with iOS/Android mobile apps.\n    37→\n    38→**User reviews:** 3.5/5 on Capterra (66 reviews). In May 2022, Allscripts sold its hospital and large physician practice business (Sunrise, Paragon, TouchWorks, dbMotion) to Harris Computer / Constellation Software for ~$700M; that segment became **Altera Digital Health**. The remaining ambulatory EHR, practice management, patient engagement, and data analytics businesses were formally rebranded as **Veradigm** in January 2023.\n\nVeradigm has ~2,450 employees and generates approximately $585M in annual revenue across two segments: Provider (~77% of revenue, covering EHR, practice management, patient engagement, and revenue cycle management) and Payer & Life Sciences (~23%, covering data analytics and real-world evidence). The company claims over 180,000 physician users. It holds approximately 3.6% of the U.S. ambulatory EHR market — significant in the small-to-mid-size independent practice segment, though small relative to Epic (~44%).\n\nVeradigm has been in financial turmoil since March 2023 when it disclosed a ~$20M revenue overstatement (2021-2022), triggering an SEC investigation, executive departures, and Nasdaq delisting in February 2024. Shares now trade OTC under ticker MDRX. The company continues operating and plans to relist after completing financial restatements, targeted for 2026. Despite the turmoil, Veradigm acquired Koha Health (RCM, orthopedic focus) in early 2024 and ScienceIO (AI data platform) for $140M.\n\nNotably, Veradigm also owns **Practice Fusion** (a separate, simpler cloud-native EHR for small practices) and **FollowMyHealth** (a patient engagement platform). These are distinct ONC-certified products. This report focuses on **Veradigm EHR**, the flagship ambulatory EHR product.\n\n## Product: Veradigm EHR\n\nCHPL ID: 11763\n\n### What It Is\n\nVeradigm EHR is the direct successor to **Allscripts Professional EHR** (also known as \"Allscripts Pro\"), which itself descended from the \"HealthMatics\" EHR. It is a full-featured ambulatory EHR targeting small-to-mid-size independent physician practices and multi-location, multi-specialty organizations. It is **not** the same product as Allscripts TouchWorks (which went to Altera Digital Health and serves larger enterprises).\n\nThe certified product (Veradigm EHR version 26) has an extensive ONC certification profile with 40+ criteria spanning clinical data management (a)(1)-(a)(15), care coordination (b)(1)-(b)(11), clinical quality measures (c)(1)-(c)(3), patient access (e)(1), public health reporting (f)(1)-(f)(7), FHIR APIs (g)(7)-(g)(10), and Direct messaging (h)(1). This breadth indicates a comprehensive clinical system.\n\nVeradigm EHR is the clinical core, but the full product ecosystem includes **Veradigm Practice Management** (billing/scheduling — separate but integrated product), **FollowMyHealth** (patient portal — EHR-agnostic, separately certified), **Veradigm ePrescribe** (also available standalone), and the **DORN** diagnostic ordering/results network. The certified EHR module is a component of this broader platform.\n\n### Users & Market\n\n**Target users:** Physicians, clinical staff, nurses, and practice administrators in ambulatory settings. The product supports solo practices through multi-site groups with 50+ providers.\n\n**Specialties:** Veradigm claims support for \"nearly every medical specialty\" with preloaded templates and protocols. Explicitly listed specialties include: cardiology, orthopedics, neurology, dermatology, gastroenterology, endocrinology, ENT, urology, family practice, internal medicine, pediatrics, OB/GYN, psychiatry, urgent care, oncology, pulmonology, rheumatology, allergy/immunology, occupational medicine, podiatry, physical therapy, and more.\n\n**Market position:** ~3.6% ambulatory market share. Ranked #1 by Black Book Research (2025) for ambulatory EHR in family practice and primary care. Over 180,000 physician users across the full Veradigm platform (not all on Veradigm EHR specifically — some use Practice Fusion).\n\n**Deployment:** Cloud-based (primary), on-premise, or hybrid. Available via web browser with iOS/Android mobile apps.\n\n**User reviews:** 3.5/5 on Capterra (66 reviews). Users praise customizability, specialty-specific templates, and lab/pharmacy integration. Common complaints include steep learning curve, slow performance (chart loading times), and variable customer support quality.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nBased on vendor website, product pages, third-party reviews, and ONC certification criteria:\n\n**Clinical Documentation & Charting**\n- One-click visit templates that auto-populate from prior visits\n- Smart Lists that learn provider ordering habits per diagnosis\n- **Ambient Scribe** (launched November 2024): AI-powered documentation that captures patient conversations and generates structured clinical notes directly in the EHR, with multilingual support\n- Voice recognition for documentation\n- Care plan creation and patient goal tracking\n- Structured diagnosis coding with HCC (Hierarchical Condition Category) alerts and RAF/risk score viewing for value-based care\n\n**ePrescribing** (also available as standalone product, Veradigm ePrescribe)\n- Integrated electronic prescribing with allergy/drug interaction checking\n- EPCS (Electronic Prescribing of Controlled Substances)\n- PDMP (Prescription Drug Monitoring Program) integration for state registries\n- **RxTruePrice**: Real-time patient-specific medication pricing showing discounted plan/PBM prices, cash prices, therapeutic alternatives, and pharmacy price comparison\n- **eAUTH**: Electronic prior authorization automation\n- Patient financial assistance and coupon information at point of care\n- Serves 80,000+ prescribers\n\n**Lab & Imaging Integration (DORN)**\n- **Veradigm DORN** (Diagnostic Ordering and Results Network): Cloud-based hub connecting to 500+ lab and radiology centers via a single API\n- Electronic ordering from within EHR workflows\n- Near real-time order status visibility\n- Results delivered directly to the EHR\n- Rapid lab integration (typically within 24 hours)\n\n**Clinical Decision Support**\n- HCC alerts for chronic condition documentation\n- Clinical Decision Insights suggesting symptoms and care plans based on practice protocols\n- AI-driven diagnosis code recommendations\n\n**Telehealth**\n- Integrated video visits with EHR documentation\n- Asynchronous \"email visits\" (questionnaire-based consultations)\n\n**Reporting & Compliance**\n- Automated reporting for PQRS, PCMH, and FQHC requirements\n- ICD-10 and CPT coding support\n- Clinical quality measure reporting (certified for CQMs)\n- Public health reporting: immunization registries, electronic case reporting, syndromic surveillance, cancer registry, antimicrobial use/resistance\n\n**Transitions of Care & Interoperability**\n- C-CDA document creation and consumption for care transitions\n- Direct messaging (h)(1) for secure health information exchange\n- FHIR R4 API access (g)(10)\n- HL7 interoperability\n- **Unity APIs** for third-party integration\n- **App Expo Marketplace** for certified third-party applications\n- Open developer portal (developer.veradigm.com)\n\n**Mobile**\n- Veradigm EHR Mobile (iOS/Android): schedule access, messaging, prescription refill management\n\n**Practice Management** (separate product: Veradigm Practice Management)\n- **Scheduling**: AI-powered \"Predictive Scheduler\" using 40+ metrics; handles walk-ins, cancellations, recurring appointments\n- **Claims processing**: 98% first-pass clean claims rate; Claim Verification for revenue leakage prevention\n- **System Rule Manager**: Real-time coding error detection and correction\n- **Office Manager**: Customizable front-office and back-office workflow queues\n- **Address Verification**: Patient contact validation via Melissa Data\n- **Revenue Cycle Management**: Central monitoring of denial rates, A/R days, scheduling metrics across sites\n- Available as software or end-to-end managed billing services\n- Capterra rating: 2.9/5 (139 reviews) — lower than the EHR itself\n- Veradigm also acquired **Koha Health** (January 2024) for orthopedic/MSK-focused RCM\n\n**Patient Portal** (separate product: FollowMyHealth)\n- EHR-agnostic, mobile-first patient engagement platform (also ONC-certified separately)\n- Personal Health Record: medical history, prescription renewals, lab results, test images\n- Secure messaging with providers\n- Online appointment scheduling with confirmations, reminders, and waitlist management\n- Mobile check-in with payment options\n- Online bill pay\n- Remote patient monitoring (device integration)\n- Caregiver proxy access\n- Post-visit satisfaction surveys\n- Medical interpretation via Voyce partnership (240+ languages plus ASL)\n- Patient-generated discrete data flows back to the EHR\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on the product's ONC certification profile and documented features, Veradigm EHR stores and manages the following data categories:\n\n**Clinical data directly evidenced by (a) criteria certification:**\n- Patient demographics (a)(5)\n- Problem lists with SNOMED CT coding (a)(1)-(a)(3)\n- Medication lists and medication allergy lists (a)(1)-(a)(3)\n- Clinical notes and encounter documentation (a)(4), (a)(14)\n- Vital signs and growth charts (a)(2)\n- Lab orders and results (via DORN integration)\n- Imaging orders and results (via DORN)\n- Implantable device data (a)(14) — UDI\n- Social determinants / social history\n- Immunization records (f)(1)\n- Smoking status\n- Family history\n- Assessment and plan of treatment\n- Care plans and patient goals\n- Health concerns\n\n**Prescribing data evidenced by ePrescribe features and (b)(3) certification:**\n- Prescription orders (active, historical, and discontinued)\n- Controlled substance prescriptions (EPCS)\n- Drug interaction and allergy alert history\n- Prior authorization records (eAUTH)\n- Medication pricing information (RxTruePrice)\n\n**Administrative data evidenced by Practice Management (separate product but integrated):**\n- Scheduling data (appointments, cancellations, recalls)\n- Insurance and coverage information\n- Claims and billing data (CPT, ICD-10 codes)\n- Payment records\n- A/R and denial tracking\n- Practice/provider configuration\n\n**Patient engagement data evidenced by FollowMyHealth (separate product):**\n- Secure messages between patient and provider\n- Patient-submitted health data (questionnaires, device data)\n- Portal access and activity logs\n- Online bill payments\n- Appointment requests\n\n**Reporting and exchange data evidenced by (b), (c), (f) criteria:**\n- C-CDA documents (sent and received)\n- CQM data and submissions\n- Public health reports (immunization, syndromic surveillance, cancer registry, electronic case reporting, antimicrobial use/resistance)\n- Audit logs (d)(2)\n\n**Important note on product boundaries:** Practice Management and FollowMyHealth are separately sold products that integrate with Veradigm EHR. Electronic Health Information (EHI) Export tool. This guide gives you\ninformation and links to additional resources for integrating the data into your organization’s software.\n\nExtracting from the .zip files\nBecause the Veradigm EHR EHI Export tool packages data in .zip files, you probably received at least one .zip file from the\npractice.\nTo unzip the file, use a third-party file archiver program that supports password protection, such as WinZip, WinRAR, or 7zip.\nYou must enter a password (provided by the practice) to extract the contents. The file extraction feature in Microsoft®\nWindows® Explorer does not support password protection, and will not work to unzip the file.\nThe file contents are one or more other .zip files, each of which corresponds with a single patient chart. In the following\nexample, the top-level .zip file from Practice ABC contains seven .zip files for seven different patients; each file is named with a\ncombination of the patient’s EHR ID and export request number. (Be aware that different Veradigm EHR practices might have\ndifferent naming conventions.)\n\nNovember 9, 2023 | Veradigm® EHR\nVersion 1 documentation for Veradigm EHR EHI Export output formatting\nCopyright © 2023 | Veradigm Inc.\nThis page contains Veradigm proprietary information and is not to be duplicated or disclosed to unauthorized persons.\n\n5\n\n\fFigure 1: Example patient .zip files (viewed in archiver program WinRAR)\n\nNote: The example above displays patient .zip files in a window of the file archiver\nprogram WinRAR. If you use a different archiver program, the .zip file icons will be\ndifferent, and the options for extracting and working with files will be different.\nTo extract the contents of an individual patient .zip file, you do not need to supply a password.\n\nNovember 9, 2023 | Veradigm® EHR\nVersion 1 documentation for Veradigm EHR EHI Export output formatting\nCopyright © 2023 | Veradigm Inc.\nThis page contains Veradigm proprietary information and is not to be duplicated or disclosed to unauthorized persons.\n\n6\n\n\fFolder hierarchy of a patient .zip file\nEach patient .zip file contains four top-level folders:\n\n\nAttachments\n\n\n\nScanned Documents\n\n\n\nTranscriptions\n\n\n\nDiscrete Data\n\nAttachments folder\nThis top-level folder contains images, documents, and voice notes that are associated with the patient’s chart. File types can\ninclude:\n .jpg, .gif, .tif, and .png\n .wav and .mp3\n PDFs\n Microsoft® Word® files\n Microsoft® Excel® files\n Microsoft® PowerPoint® files\n And many more\nIf no attachments exist for a patient, the folder will simply be empty.\n\nScanned Documents folder\nThis top-level folder also contains documents that are associated with the patient’s chart. Called scanned documents, they\ndiffer from other files saved to a patient chart because they originated as hard-copy documents that were scanned into the\nEHR through a component called Input Manager. All scanned documents are of file type .tif.\nNote that multi-page scanned documents are saved as a sequence of .tif files, each with the same numerical prefix. * Extract field definitions from Veradigm View V6 EHI Export Documentation HTML pages.\n *\n * Each page documents a single TSV file with a table of fields (Field Name, Data type, Field Description).\n * This script parses all 87 HTML pages and emits a unified JSON catalog.\n *\n * Run: bun run extract-view-v6-tables.ts\n */\n\nimport { readdirSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync } from \"fs\";\nimport { join } from \"path\";\n\nconst VIEW_DIR = join(__dirname, \"..\", \"veradigm-view-v6\");\nconst OUT_FILE = join(__dirname, \"view-v6-catalog.json\");\nconst ACCOUNTING_FILE = join(__dirname, \"view-v6-accounting.json\");\n\ninterface Field {\n  name: string;\n  dataType: string;\n  description: string;\n}\n\ninterface TsvDefinition {\n  slug: string;\n  tsvFilename: string;\n  entityDescription: string;\n  section: string | null;\n  fields: Field[];\n  sourceFile: string;\n}\n\n// Map slugs to sections based on the index page structure\nconst SECTION_MAP: Record<string, string> = {};\nconst INDEX_SECTIONS: { section: string; slugs: string[] }[] = [\n  {\n    section: \"Demographics\",\n    slugs: [\n      \"communication-settings\",  },\n];\n\nfor (const { section, slugs } of INDEX_SECTIONS) {\n  for (const slug of slugs) {\n    SECTION_MAP[slug] = section;\n  }\n}\n\nfunction extractTableFromHtml(html: string): { title: string; description: string; fields: Field[] } {\n  // Extract the h2 title (TSV filename)\n  const h2Match = html.match(/<h2[^>]*>([^<]+)<\\/h2>/);\n  const title = h2Match ? h2Match[1].trim() : \"\";\n\n  // Extract the h4 description\n  const h4Match = html.match(/<h4[^>]*>([^<]+)<\\/h4>/);\n  const description = h4Match ? h4Match[1].trim() : \"\";\n\n  // Extract table rows\n  const fields: Field[] = [];\n  const tbodyMatch = html.match(/<tbody>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/tbody>/);\n  if (tbodyMatch) {\n    const rows = tbodyMatch[1].matchAll(/<tr>\\s*<td>([^<]*)<\\/td>\\s*<td>([^<]*)<\\/td>\\s*<td>([^<]*)<\\/td>\\s*<\\/tr>/g);\n    for (const row of rows) {\n      fields.push({\n        name: row[1].trim(),\n        dataType: row[2].trim(),\n        description: row[3].trim(),\n      });\n    }\n  }\n\n  return { title, description, fields };\n}\n\nconst files = readdirSync(VIEW_DIR).filter(f => f.endsWith(\".html\") && f !== \"index.html\").sort();\n\nconst definitions: TsvDefinition[] = [];\nconst failures: { file: string; error: string }[] = [];\n\nfor (const file of files) {\n  const slug = file.replace(\".html\", \"\");\n  const filePath = join(VIEW_DIR, file);\n\n  try {\n    const html = readFileSync(filePath, \"utf-8\");\n    const { title, description, fields } = extractTableFromHtml(html);\n\n    if (fields.length === 0) {\n      failures.push({ file, error: \"No fields extracted from table\" });\n      continue;\n    }\n\n    definitions.push({\n {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01Y5owZSHf2FDoiTccLiYtK6","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01K8kWXCpEPvonqZgoDr3jK3","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"cat > /home/jmandel/hobby/ehi-export-analysis/results/veradigm--veradigm-ehr/ehi-export-report.md << 'REPORTEOF'\n# Veradigm — EHI Export Documentation\n\nCollected: 2026-02-16\n\n## Source\n- Registered URL: https://veradigm.com/legal/onc-reg-compliance/\n- CHPL IDs: 11763\n- Product: Veradigm EHR, Version 26\n- Certification date: 2025-12-31\n\n## Navigation Journal\n\n1. **Initial probe**: `curl -sI -L \"https://veradigm.com/legal/onc-reg-compliance/\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0'` returned HTTP 200, Content-Type: text/html, 250KB. Static HTML served from S3/CloudFront.\n\n2. **Page examination**: The page is a comprehensive ONC regulatory compliance hub for all Veradigm products. It contains sections for mandatory disclosures, information blocking complaints, cost transparency, real-world testing, and EHI export documentation.\n\n3. **EHI Export Documentation section found**: Under the heading \"EHI Export Documentation\" with introductory text: *\"Documentation on the 170.315 (b)(10) EHI Export output format for each Veradigm ONC certified solution is provided below.\"*\n\n4. **Documentation organized by product**:\n   - **Veradigm EHR**: 1 PDF (v1, Nov 2023)\n   - **Veradigm ePrescribe**: 1 PDF (v1, Nov 2023)\n   - **Veradigm FollowMyHealth**: 1 PDF (v2, Feb 2024) + FHIR endpoint serving v1 PDF\n   - **Veradigm Practice Management** (non-ONC certified): 2 PDFs (v1 Dec 2023, v2 May 2024)\n   - **Veradigm View**: 6 versions of web-based documentation (V1-V6), latest V6 published Jan 2026\n\n5. **PDF downloads**: All PDFs downloaded directly via `curl` with no authentication required:\n   ```\n   curl -sL \"https://veradigm.com/img/legal/onc/VeradigmEHR_EHI_Export_output_format_documentation_v1.pdf\"\n   curl -sL \"https://veradigm.com/img/legal/onc/VeradigmePrescribe_EHI_Export_Documentation_v1.pdf\"\n   curl -sL \"https://veradigm.com/img/legal/onc/VeradigmFMH_EHI_Export_Data_Guide_v2.pdf\"\n   curl -sL \"https://veradigm.com/img/legal/onc/EHIDataExportFile_ReferenceGuide_VeradigmPM_V2.pdf\"\n   curl -sL \"https://veradigm.com/img/legal/onc/EHIDataExportFile_ReferenceGuide_VeradigmPM.pdf\"\n   ```\n\n6. **FollowMyHealth FHIR endpoint**: `https://fhir.followmyhealth.com/documentation/EHIExportDocumentation/v1` serves a PDF (v1, 18 pages). The v2 endpoint serves the same PDF as the v2 download from the main page. Also fetched 9 custom FHIR StructureDefinitions, CodeSystems, and ValueSets referenced in the FMH PDF from `fhir.followmyhealth.com/api/`.\n\n7. **Veradigm View V6 documentation**: Index page at `https://veradigm.com/legal/veradigm-view-ehi-export-documentation/v6/index/` contains links to 87 subpages, each documenting a single TSV file's field definitions. All 87 pages downloaded. Note: these pages reference \"Practice Fusion EHR\" — Veradigm View is the rebranded documentation for Practice Fusion, a separate Veradigm product.\n\n## What Was Found\n\nVeradigm provides **separate EHI export documentation for five different products** within its ecosystem. The core EHR product (CHPL 11763) has its own dedicated 89-page PDF. The related products (ePrescribe, FollowMyHealth, Practice Management, and Veradigm View/Practice Fusion) each have their own documentation.\n\n### Veradigm EHR Export (Primary — CHPL 11763)\n\nThe EHR export produces **password-protected .zip files** containing one nested .zip per patient. Each patient zip contains four top-level folders:\n\n1. **Attachments** — Images, documents, voice notes (.jpg, .gif, .tif, .png, .wav, .mp3, .pdf, .doc, .xls, .ppt, etc.)\n2. **Scanned Documents** — Scanned hard-copy documents (.tif files) ingested via Input Manager\n3. **Transcriptions** — Clinical transcriptions in .rtf format\n4. **Discrete Data** — Structured clinical data in **JSON** files organized by clinical domain\n\nThe Discrete Data folder contains JSON files for **17 clinical domains**:\n- **Demographics** (19 fields including PatientID, name, DOB, SSN, gender, race, ethnicity, language, address, phone, email, emergency contact, employer, marital status, smoking status)\n- **History** (social history, medical history, surgical history, family history — documented with SnomedCode, ICD codes)\n- **Vitals** (height, weight, BMI, temperature, blood pressure, pulse, respiratory rate, pulse oximetry, head circumference, pain severity)\n- **Diagnosis** (active/inactive/resolved diagnoses with SNOMED, ICD-9, ICD-10 codes, onset dates, providers)\n- **Medications** (prescribed medications with NDC, RxNorm, route, dose, frequency, start/end dates, prescriber, pharmacy)\n- **Procedures** (CPT, SNOMED, ICD-10-PCS codes, procedure dates, providers)\n- **Lab Orders** (ordering details, LOINC codes, performing lab, specimen info, order status)\n- **Referrals** (referral details, referring/referred-to providers, status)\n- **Flowsheet** (custom flowsheet data entries)\n- **Risk Management Program** (patient enrollment in risk management programs)\n- **Contact** (patient contact records)\n- **Encounter** (encounter details including type, date, reason, location, providers, status; linked to encounter-level diagnoses, vitals, procedures)\n- **ReasonForVisit** (chief complaints with SNOMED codes)\n- **Immunization** (vaccine details with CVX, MVX codes, lot numbers, administration details, VIS dates)\n- **Message** (clinical messages/notes between providers and patients)\n- **Questionnaire** (structured questionnaire responses)\n- **Care Plans** (care plan goals, interventions, target dates, status)\n\nAdditional JSON files:\n- **ChartTranscriptions.json** — Metadata about transcription files\n- **ChartAttachments.json** — Metadata about chart attachments with LOINC codes and document types\n\nEach JSON file documents: field names, descriptions, and SQL data types (VARCHAR, Integer, Date, Datetimeoffset, etc.). The documentation maps to internal EHR database table names.\n\n### Veradigm ePrescribe Export (Separate product)\n\nExports patient data as **TSV (tab-separated value) files** in zip packages. Six TSV files per patient:\n- Allergies.tsv, Demographics.tsv, Diagnosis.tsv, Historical Medications.tsv, Insurance.tsv, Prescriptions.tsv\n\nThis is a narrow clinical subset appropriate for a standalone ePrescribing product.\n\n### Veradigm FollowMyHealth Export (Separate product)\n\nExports in **FHIR R4 JSON** format as a zip file. Supports 17 FHIR resource types:\nAccount, AllergyIntolerance, Appointment, Bundle, Communication, Condition, DiagnosticReport, DocumentReference, Encounter, FamilyMemberHistory, Immunization, Invoice, Medication, MedicationRequest, Observation, Patient, Practitioner, Procedure.\n\nUses custom FHIR extensions for allergy status, health condition status, family history, and procedure status (StructureDefinitions and CodeSystems hosted at fhir.followmyhealth.com).\n\n### Veradigm Practice Management Export (Non-ONC-certified)\n\nExports patient **financial/billing data** as a single JSON file per patient. The structure is hierarchical: patient → vouchers (claims) → services → payments. Includes detailed billing fields: procedure codes, modifiers, diagnoses, payer info, claim information fields (prior auth, condition codes, occurrence codes, value codes), plus specialty appendices for ambulance, drug, anesthesia, and dental billing fields.\n\n### Veradigm View / Practice Fusion Export (Separate product)\n\nExports as **TSV files** with 87 documented table definitions across 8 categories:\n- Demographics (10 TSVs), Patient (2), Clinical (32), Billing and insurance (12), Medications and prescriptions (11), Labs (15), Referrals (2), Messaging (3)\n- Total: 1,194 documented fields\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\n**Focusing on Veradigm EHR (CHPL 11763):**\n\nThe Veradigm EHR export is a **genuine b(10) export** — not just a FHIR API repackaging. It exports data in vendor-native JSON format with direct database table mappings, and includes binary attachments (images, scanned documents, transcriptions). This goes well beyond the USCDI/US Core subset.\n\n**Clearly covered domains:**\n- Demographics (comprehensive — includes SSN, employer, emergency contacts, language preferences)\n- Problem lists / diagnoses (with SNOMED, ICD-9, ICD-10 coding)\n- Medications (with NDC, RxNorm, pharmacy details)\n- Allergies (documented in the History section)\n- Lab orders and results (detailed LOINC-coded lab data)\n- Vital signs (full vitals panel)\n- Immunizations (CVX/MVX coded with administration details)\n- Procedures (CPT, SNOMED, ICD-10-PCS coded)\n- Clinical notes/transcriptions (via Transcriptions folder and message JSON)\n- Encounter documentation (rich encounter records with linked diagnoses, procedures, vitals)\n- Care plans and goals\n- Referrals\n- Family history\n- Social history / smoking status\n- Chart attachments and scanned documents (binary files included)\n- Questionnaire responses / assessments\n- Risk management program enrollment\n\n**Domains that appear to be missing or ambiguous from the EHR export:**\n- **Billing/claims data**: The EHR export itself does not include billing data. This is documented separately in the Practice Management export. Since PM is listed as \"non ONC certified\" and is a separate product, this is expected — billing lives in PM, not EHR. However, for practices running both, a complete patient record export would need both.\n- **Insurance/coverage information**: Not present in the EHR export. Available in the PM and ePrescribe exports.\n- **ePrescribe-specific data**: Prescription transaction details (eRx routing, pharmacy responses) are in the ePrescribe product, not the EHR export.\n- **Patient portal data**: Secure messages, patient-submitted data, and portal activity are in FollowMyHealth, not the EHR.\n- **Specialty-specific clinical data**: The documentation mentions \"Flowsheet\" and \"Questionnaire\" domains which may capture some specialty-specific data, but there's no explicit mention of specialty-specific structured data (e.g., ophthalmology visual acuity, dermatology lesion maps, cardiology EKG data). Given Veradigm EHR claims support for \"nearly every medical specialty,\" the lack of documented specialty-specific export fields is a gap worth noting.\n\n**Important architectural note:** Veradigm's approach of having separate exports for EHR, PM, ePrescribe, and FollowMyHealth means that **no single export captures the complete patient record** for practices using the integrated suite. A practice using Veradigm EHR + PM + FollowMyHealth would need to perform three separate exports and merge the results to get a complete picture.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe Veradigm EHR export uses a **proprietary JSON format** mapped directly to internal database tables. This is an appropriate choice for a b(10) export — it captures data the way the system stores it, without lossy transformation to a standard that might not accommodate all fields.\n\nThe JSON structure is well-documented with:\n- Field names matching internal database column names\n- SQL data types specified (VARCHAR(n), Integer, Date, Datetimeoffset)\n- Internal database table names provided\n- Primary key fields identified\n- Coded fields include code system references (SNOMED, ICD-9, ICD-10, CVX, MVX, LOINC, NDC, RxNorm)\n\nBinary content (images, scanned documents, transcriptions, voice notes) is included as actual files alongside the JSON data — a significant plus that many vendors omit.\n\nA third party could reconstruct the patient record from this export, though they would need to understand the relationships between JSON files (e.g., encounter IDs linking encounters to encounter-level diagnoses, procedures, and vitals). Relationship documentation is implicit through shared key fields rather than explicitly diagrammed.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- The 89-page EHR PDF is substantial and well-organized\n- Every field has a name, description, and data type\n- Database table names and primary keys are documented\n- The folder/file hierarchy is clearly explained with examples\n- Screenshots show the zip structure\n- JSON format is explained with links to learning resources\n- The documentation is clearly written and navigable\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- The EHR export documentation is **version 1 from November 2023** and has not been updated since. Given the product version is 26 (certified Dec 2025), the export documentation may lag behind the current product.\n- No sample export files or worked examples of JSON content are provided (only folder structure screenshots)\n- Coded value sets are referenced by system (SNOMED, ICD, etc.) but not enumerated — for fields like \"Status\" (VARCHAR), the valid values are not documented\n- Relationships between tables/JSON files are not explicitly documented (no ERD or relationship diagram)\n- No API documentation for triggering the export programmatically\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\nThe EHR export documentation provides **field-level granularity** for 17 clinical domains plus 2 metadata files. Each field has:\n- Field name (matching internal column names)\n- Human-readable description\n- SQL data type with length constraints\n\n**What's documented well:** Field names, descriptions, data types, folder hierarchy, file naming conventions.\n\n**What's missing:**\n- Cardinality/optionality (which fields are required vs. optional)\n- Enumerated value sets for coded fields\n- Explicit relationship/foreign key documentation between tables\n- Versioning/change history\n- Sample data files\n\nThe granularity is good for understanding what data is in the export but insufficient for building a robust import system without trial-and-error with actual export files.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://veradigm.com/legal/onc-reg-compliance/\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (all content accessible via curl)\n- Navigation complexity: one_click (EHI section clearly labeled on compliance page)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n\nNone significant. All documentation was publicly accessible without authentication. The only minor issue was that the FollowMyHealth FHIR documentation endpoint (fhir.followmyhealth.com) returns a 405 to HEAD requests but works fine with GET.\nREPORTEOF\necho \"Report written\"","description":"Write EHI export report"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":603,"cache_read_input_tokens":119058,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":603},"output_tokens":2,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"18d421ae-18b3-4792-aa5b-9dcecf11b702","uuid":"3613286f-9421-4cd3-9eb0-2af86eedc97b"}
